*Sediment can be divided into two general categories.*
*One kind has slowly absorbed the mana present in the ground over a long period of time—'natural sediment'.*
*It's practically just rock that's absorbed a mixture of all five kinds of mana: pyro, aqua, terra, aero, and void.*
*The other kind is natural sediment which has undergone a treatment process, allowing it to be filled with mana.*
*These used in manakravte, and referred to as 'kravter's sediment'.*
*When referring to sediment, it's usually the latter.*
*Once filled with mana, kravter's sediment is ranked according to how much of the mana stored inside remains unchanged.*
*For a low quality cut, such as class 3 or 4, mana put into the stone will change into another kind regardless of how pure it originally was.*
*This is referred to as 'impurification'.*
*For example, even after filling a stone with 100% aqua mana, it might turn into an 80-20 mix of aqua and terra.*
*Being exposed for a long time to the same type of mana will end up leaving it slightly aligned towards that type of mana.*
*This makes achieving the required ratios for kravtes harder, and no amount of processing will help, which is why the class is lower.*
*Hence, it's common practice that class 1 sediment is filled only with 100% purified mana when sold.*
*Purified mana, not having any attribute, is the easiest to impurify.*
*To prove that a cut of sediment is of a high level of purity, filling it with purified mana is the fastest way to go about it.*
Ritona: "......"
*The sediment that the store's manager referred to as class 1 had some slight impurities in it...*
*This normally would never be classified as class 1.*
*Probably closer to class 2.*
Ritona: "...Hmm."
*Well, even putting all of that pretentious monologue aside...*
*Not the best way to put it, but...*
*Seeing this strange juxtaposition of exquisitely made cases like these stuffed with sediment with quality this poor... it's laughable.*
*It's like... stuffing a solid gold case with a pile of acorns that some child picked off the ground.*
Ritona: "The only one worth anything seems to be... this class 3 cut."
*This appears to be the one most true to the given class.*

*Sediment is processed in such a way that when filled with mana, the mana can be retained without seeping out.*
*This process is referred to as a 'commit'. The way it's tied off, so to speak, shows you the kind of character of whoever did the processing.*
